MINISTRY - OF FINANCE 
(Department of Revenue) 

[Office of the Director General of Income-tax 
(Exemptions)] 

Calcutta, the 16th October, 1990 
(INCOME TAX) 

S.O. 3187.— It is hereby notified for seneinl infotnmuon 
that the organisation mentioned below has been approved by 


the Prescribed Authority under Rule 6 of the Income-tax 
Rules, 1962, i.e., the Director General of Income-tax Exemp¬ 
tions) in concurrence with the Secretary, Department of 
Scientific and Industrial Research for the purposes of clause 
(ii) of sub-section (1) of Section 35 (Thirty Five/one/two) 
ot the income-tax Act, 1961 under the categories "Institu¬ 
tion” subject to the following conditions : 

ti) The organisation wii) maintain a separate account 
of the sums received by it for Scientific Research ; 

(ii) It will furnish the Annual Return of its scientific 

research activities to the Secretary, Department of 
Scientific and Industrial Research, 'Technology 
Bliawan, New Mehrauli Road, New Delhi-110016 
lor every financial year by 31st May of each year ; 
and 

(iii) It will submit to the (a; Director General of Income- 

tax (Exemptions), (b) Secretary, Department of 
Scientific and Industrial Research and (c) Commis¬ 
sioner of lncome-tax/Pirector of Income-tax (Exem¬ 
ptions) having jurisdiction over the organisation, by 
the 30th June each year a copy of its audited 
annual accounts showing its income and expenditure, 
and its assets and liabilities. 

NAME OF THE ORGANISATION 

The Spastics Society of India Opp. Afghan Church, 
Upper Colaba Road, Colabu, Bombay-400005. 

This Nolilication is elleeiive for the period from 1-4-1990 
to 31-3 1992. 

Note.—The organisation is advised to apply (in triplicate) 

. for further extension of the approval, to the Director 
Getneral of' Income-tax (Exemptions), Calcutta 
through the Commissioner of Income-taxi the Director 
of Income-tax (Exemptions) having jurisdiction o\er 
the organisation, three months before the e.vp’ry of 
the approval. In exceptional cases where the order 
granting approval js received after the expiry of 
the period of three months aforesaid or shortly 
before the expiry of the said period, tho organisa¬ 
tion may make an application for extension of ap¬ 
proval as soon as possible after the receipt of the 
order of approval. Six copies of the application 
lor extension of approval should be sent directly 
to the Secretary, Department of Scientific and Indus¬ 
trial Research.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 8th November, 1990 

S.O. 3198.—M/s. Autofield Engineering Pvt. Ltd. Sabaj- 
pur, Nanddur, Taluka Daimd, Distt. Pune (Maharashtra) 
were granted an import licence No. PjDj2280234 dt, 20-6-90 
for Rs. 53,52,000 (Rupees Ffty three I.akhs and fifty two 
thousands only) for import of SS Bars as per list attached. 

2. The firm has applied for issue of duplicate copy of im¬ 
port licence has been lost or misplaced. It has further boeti 
stated that the import licence was not registered with any 
Customs Authority. 

3. In support of their contention, the licensee has failed 
and affidavit on stamped paper duly sworn in before a 
Notary Public Madras, I am according satisfied that the 
original import licence (Exchange Control Copy only) 
No. P[D|2280234 dt. 20-6-90 has been lost or misplaced by 
the firm. In exercise of the powers conferred under sub¬ 


clause 9(cc) of the Import (Control) Order 1955 dt. 7-12-1955 
as amended the said original import licence No. P[D|2280234 
dt. 20-6-90 issued to JVijs. Autofidd Engineers (Pvt.) Ltd. 
Pune is hereby cancelled. 

4. A duplicate import licence (Fxchange Control copy) 

Is being issued to the party separately,

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 12th November, 1990 

S.O. 3199.—M/s. Huricane Stud Farm, 19/1, Vittal 
Mallya Road, BaDgalorc-560001 were granted an Import 
Licence No. P|P[0516632 dated 31-8-90 for Rs. 13,00,000 
(Rupees Thirecn Lakhs) for import of One Stallion and 
one Broodmare Under Para 110(i) of Current 1990—93 
Import Policy. 

It has now been found that the said Import (Licence was 
issued inadverlantly to M/s, Huricane Stud Farm, Bangalore. 

I am satisfied that the Import Licence No, P|P|0516632 
dated 31-8-90 for Rs. 13,00,000 was issued inadvertantly. In 
exercise of the powers conferred under sub-clause 9(a) of the 
Import Control Order, 1965, as amended, I hereby cancel 
the Import Licence No. P/P 0516632 dated 31-8-90. 

[No. 4/8/AM-91/ALS]

S.O. 3202,—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Natural Gqs from Boris, Tahsil Alibag, District 
Raigad to Village SaUv, Tahsil Murud (Janjira), District 
Raigad in tire State of Maharashtra, ripe Line should be 
laid through the Agency of Grasirn Industries Ltd., 91, 
Sakhar Bhavan, Naiiman Point, Bombay-400021, 

Aud whereas, it appears to the Central Government 
that for the purpose of laying such Pipe Lines, it is neces¬ 
sary to acquire the right of user in the land described in 
the schedule annexed hereto :—

S.O. 3203.--‘Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Natural Gas fiom Boris, Tahsil Alibag, District 
Raigad to Village Salav, Tahsil Murud (Janjira), District 
Raigad in the State of Maharashtra, Pipe Line should be 
laid through the Agency of Grasim Industries Ltd,, 91, 
Sakhar Bhavan, Nariman Point, Bombay-400021, 

And whereas, it appears to the Central Government 
that for the purpose of laying such Pipe Lines, it is neces¬ 
sary to acquire the light of User in the land described in 
the schedule annexed hereto:— 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 


Pipe Lines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares it’s intention to acquire the right of user in the 
lands referred in the Schedule: 

Provided that any person interested in the said lands 
having any objection for laying the Pipe Lines through 
the said lands may prefer any objection within 21 days 
from the date of the Notification, to the Competent Autho¬ 
rity, Grasim Industries Ltd,, Prnbhu Building, Alibag, 
District Raigad, Maharashtra State 

And every person making such an objection shall state 
specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
a legal practitioner.

S.O. 3204.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Natural Gas from Boris. Tahsil Alibag, District 
Raigad to Village Salav, Tahsil Murud (Janjlra), District 
Raigad in the State of Maharashtra, Pipe Line should he 
laid through the Agency of Grasini Industries Ltd., 91, 
Sakhar Bhavan, Nariman Point, Bombay-400021. 

And, whereas, it appears to the Central Government 
that for the purpose of laying such Pipe Lines, it is neces¬ 
sary to acquire the light of user in the land described in 
the schedule annexed hereto. 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred hy 
Sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 


Pipe Lines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (50 of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares it’s intention to acquire the right of user in the 
lands referred in the Schedule : 

Provided that any person interested in the said lands 
having any objection for laying the Pipe Lines through 
the said lands may orefer any objection within 21 days 
from the date of the Notilication, to the Competent Autho¬ 
rity, Grasim Industries Ltd., Prabhu Building, Alibag, 
District Raigad, Maharashtra State, 

And every person making such an objection shall state 
specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
a legal practitioner.

S.O. 3206.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the 
transport of Natural Gas from Boris, Tahsil Alibag, District 
Raigad to Village Salav, Tahsil Muru.l (Janjira), District 
Raigad in the State of Mahai ashtra, pipe Tine should be 
laid through the Agency of Grasmi Industries Ltd., 91, 
Sakhar Bhavan, Nariman Point, Bombay-400021, 

And whereas, it appears to the Central Government 
that for the purpose of laying such Pipe Lines, it is neces¬ 
sary to acquire the right of user in the land described m 
the schedule annexed hereto :— 


Now', therefore, In exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the petroleum and Minerals 


Pipe Lines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (JO of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares it’s intention to acquire the right of user in the 
lands referred In the Schedule 

Provided that any person interested in the said lands 
having any objection for laying the Pipe Lines through 
the said lands may prefer any objection within 21 days 
from the date of the Notification, to the Competent Autho¬ 
rity, Grasim Industries Ltd,, Prabhu Building, Al.bag, 
District Raigad, Maharashtra State. 

And every person making such an objection shall state 
specifically whether he wishes to be beard in person or by 
a legal practitioner.

Rnigad to Village £u!av, Tahsil Murud (Janjira), Disliict

laid through the Agency of Orasitn Industries Ltd., 9). 
Sakhar Bhavnn, Nariman Point, Bombay-400021. 

And whereas, it aopcavs to the Central Government 
that for the purpose of laying such Pipe Lines, it is neces¬ 
sary to acquire the right of user in the land described in 
the schedule annexed hereto 

Now, therefore, in exorcise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-section (1 ) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals

Pipe Lines (Acquisition of Right of User iu the Land) 
Act, 1962 (JO of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares it’s intention to acquire the light of user in the

ment that it is necessary in the public interest that “ftlie 
transport of Natural Gas from Bous, Tahsil Alibag, District 
Raigad to Village Saiav, Tahsil Murud (Janjlra) District

sflry to acquire the fight Of user in the land described in 
the schedule annexed hereto

Sub-section (1) of Section 3.of the Petroleum -and Minerals

declares it’s intention to acquire the right of user in the 
lands referred in the Schedule: 

Provided that any person interested in !he said lands

specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by

S.O. 3214.—Whereas it appears to the Central Govern¬ 
ment that it is nccessury in (lie public interest that for me 
transport of Natural Gas, from Boris, Taltsil Alibag, District 
Raigtal to Village Salav, I'ahsil Murucl (Janjira), District 
Raigatl in the State of Maharashtra, pipe Line should be 
laid through the Agency of Grasirn Industries Ltd., 91. 
Sakhar Bhavan, Nariman Point, Bombay-400021. 

And whereas, it appears to the Central Government 
that for the purpose of laying such Pipe Lines, it is neces¬ 
sary to acquire the right of user in the land described in 
the schedule annexed hereto: — 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
dub-section (1> of Section 3 .of the Petroleum and M nerals 


Pipe Lines (Acquisition of Right ot User in the Land) 
Act, 1962 (5o of 1962), the Central Government hereby 
declares it's intention to acquire the right of user in the 
lands referred in the Schedule 

Provided that any person interested in (he said lands 
having any objection for laying the Pipe Lines through 
the said lands may prefer any objection within 21 days 
from the date of the Notification, to the Competent Autho¬ 
rity, Grasirn Industries Ltd., Prabhu Building, Al.bag, 
District Raigad, Maharashtra State. 

And every person making such an objection shall state 
specifically whether he wishes to be heard in person or by 
a legal practitioner.

ment that it is necessary in the public interest that for the

laid through the Aecncy of Grasim Industries Ltd., 91, 
aakhar Bhavan, Nariman Point, Bombay-400021, 

And whereas, it appears to the Central Government 
that for the purpose of laying such Pipe Lines, it is neces¬ 
sary to acquire the right o': user in the land described in 
the schedule annexed hereto ;— 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
Sub-section (1) of Section 3 of the Petroleum and Minerals 
Pipe Lines (Acquisition of Right of User in the Land) 


['ET.tf. ^,-14016/40/88-^. "ft. ( 14 )]

declares it’s intention to acquire the light of user in the 
lands referred in the Schedule 

Provided that any person interested in the said lands 
having any objection for laying the Pipe Lines through

rity, Grasim Industries Ltd., Prabhu Building, Alibag,

S.O. 3228.—Whereas it appears to the Central Government that coal is likely to be obtained 
from the lands mentioned in the Schedule hereto annexed; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section (1) of section 4 of the Coal Bearing 
Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957), the Central Government hereby gives notice 
of its intention to prospect for coal therein, 

The Plan bearing no. SECE/BSP/CGM/LER/JLand/70 dated the 13th July, 1990 of the area covered 
by this notification can be inspected at the office of the South Eastern Coalfields Limited (Revenue Section), 
Seepat Road, Bilaspur or at the office of the Collector, Bilaspur (Madhya Pradesh) or at the office of the 
Coal Controller, 1 Council House Street, Calcutta,

documents referred to in sub-seetiou (7) of section 3 of the said Act to the Deputy Chief Estate Manager, 
South Eastern Coalfields Limited, Seeput Road, Bilaspur (Madhya Pradesh) within ninety days from the 
date of publication of this notification in the Official Gazette.

New Dellii, the 14th November, 1990 

S.O. 3231.—Whereas it appears to the Central Government that coal in likly to be obtained from 
the lands mentioned in the Schedule hereto annexed; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub-section U) of section 4 of the Coal Gearij ^ 
Areas (Acquisition and Devlopnient) Act, 1957 (20 of 1957), the Central G ovrnment hereby gives notice of 
its intention to prospect for coal therein. 

The plan (Drawing No. SD/55 dated 19th May, 1990 of the area covered by this notification may be 
inspected in the office of the Deputy Commissioner, District Godda(Bihar) or in the office of the Coal Con¬ 
troller, 1 , Council House Street, Calcutta or in the office of the Director Technical (West Division) Eastern 
Coalfields Limited. Sanetoria. Post Office Dishergarh, District Burdwan (West Bengal). 



5154 THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : DECEMBER 1, 1990/AGRAHAYANA 10, 1912 [Part II— Sec. 3(ii)] 

All persons interested in the lands coverd by this notification shall deliver all maps, charts and other, 
documents referred to in sub-section (7) of section 13 of the said Act to the Director Technical (West Division) 
Eastern Coalfields Limited, Sanctoria, Post Office Dishergarh, District Burdwan (West Bengal) within 
ninety days from the date of the publication of this notification in the Official Gazette.

(Delhi Division) 

New Delhi, the 6th November, 1990 

S.O. 3244,—Whereas certain modifications, which the 
Central Government proposes to make in the Master Plan 
for Delhi/Zonal Development Plan regarding the areas men¬ 
tioned hereunder, were published with Notice No. F. 20(17)/ 
87'MP dated 24-6-89 in accordance with the provisions of 
Section 44 of the Delhi Development Act, 1957 (61 of 1957), 
inviting objections/suggestions as required by sub-section (3) 
of Section 11-A of the said Act, within thirty days from the 
date of the said notice ; 

And whereas no objection/suggestions were received from 
the public with regard to the said proopsed modifications 
and whereas the Central Government have decided to modify 
the Master Plan for Delhi/Zonal Development Plan ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
sub-section (2) of Section 11-A of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby makes the following modification in the 
said Master Plan for Delhi with effect from the date of 
publication of this Notification in the Gazette of India. 


NOTIFICATIONS : 

(i) The land use of an area measuring about 4.84 acres 

falling in Zone C-l 1 end bounded by Regional Park 
towards North, existing Hindu Rao Hospital towards 
South 60’ R/N road towards East and Regional 
Park towards West is changed from Recreational 
(Ridge Area) to “Public and Semi-Public facilities” 
(Hospital) subject to the condition that no construc¬ 
tion will come up in this area and the land will be 
kept green in its natural environment. 

(ii) The land use of an area measuring about 1.49 acres 

falling in zone C-ll and bounded by existing Hindu 
Rao Hospital towards North, Regional Park towards 
South, 60’ R/W road towards East and Regional 
Park towards West is changed from “Recreational” 
(Ridge Area) to “Public and Semi-Public facilities 


(Hospital)” subject to the condition that no cons¬ 
truction will come up in this area and the land will 
be kept green in its natural environ. 

[No. K-13011 |7|88-DDVA|IB] 
ARJAN DEV, Under Secy.

Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes Ihe award of the Industrial Tribunal, 
Guvvahati as shown in the Annexure, in the industrial dispute 
between the employers in rclanon to the management of 
Guwahati Refinery, Noonmati, Guwahati and their workmen, 
which was received by the Central Government on 6-11-1990.

AWARD 


This Reference arising out of the Central Government 
Notification No. L-3001J / IC/86-D.UI.B/IV (B) dated 
12-10-87 has been originally referred lo the Central Govern¬ 
ment Industrial 'tribunal, Calculi- 1 but subsequently U was 
transferred to this Tribunal by Central Government under 
Section 33-B of the Industrial Dispute Act vide its uotifica- 
tion No. S-l 1025(2)/8S-D.IV (B) dated 10-3-88. The matters 
for adjudication are as follows :— 

“Whether the action of the Management of Guwahati 
Refinery in not paying overtime at the double 
rate to the workers working beyond 36 hours 
per week and secondly not paying overtime R> 
the categories of workers such as Accountants/ 
Cashiers/Assistant Cheniist/Engincering Assistant 
Grade I and Gradr-JI in the scale of pay Rs. 640— 
1425 and Rs, 700 to Rs. 1659 involving 200 clerical' 
staff and 20 daffi respectively,, is justilied 7 If not, 
to what relief the workmen are entitled r }” 

After receipt of the -ecoids from the Central Government 
Industrial Tribunal, Calcutta the parties were asked to file 
their written statements, Parties _ files their written state¬ 
ments. The union also files a rejoinder. 


When the case fixed tor hearing parlies want time to 
settle the dispute amicably. Accordingly parties file a joint 
petition with the terms of settlement arrived at by the parties 
and prays for an award as per terms of the ;ettlement. It 
appears the terms of settlement aro just, reasonable and 
fair and in accordance with law. Hence reference is .awarded 
as per terms of the settlement marked ‘X’ which will form 
part of the award. 

I give this Award on this 26th day of October, 1990 at 
Guwahati under my hand and seal.

D. N. Hazarika, Presiding Officer, Industrial Tribunal,

Vmorandum of settlement by and between the Management 
r Guwahati Rcfitierv and their workmen represented by 
iuwahati Refinery Workers’ Union, A Registered Trade 




52SU THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : DECEMBER 1, 1 990/AGRAHAYANA 10, 1912 [Part II— Sec. 3(G)] 


Union being Registration No, 505 

The Guwahati Refinery workers’ Union which represents 
the workmen employed m the Guwahati Refinery has been 
agitating since some time back for payment of over time wages 
to the non-ofllcer supervisory staff such as Accountants, 
Cashiers, Asstt. Chemists, Engineering Asstt, C)r. 1 and 11, 
Office Superintendent, Administration, transport, Canteen 
and Guest House Supervisor lor treating them as workmen 
under the Standing Orders in force in the Guwahati Refinery 
and also for payment of Over Time wages to them that 
may be required to pay for doiug overtime according to the 
need of the management. The said demands not having 
settled, a reference has been mad to the industrial Tribunal, 
Guwahati by Notification No. S-ll/25(2)y 88-D.IV/B dated 
10-3-88. The said dispute has been registered as Reference 
No. 5-c of m8. In the notification the issue referred is as 
follows : 

The said issue has two parties. The first part relates 
to a demand regarding payment of overtime wages to the 
workmen who works 36 hours a week. The said part fur¬ 
ther mentions the demand as payment of overtime at double 
the rate of normal wages to the said workmen who works 
36 hours a week. The second part of the issue relates to 
payment of over time wages to the non-officer supervisory 
staff as mentioned earlier. The matter was bilaterally discus¬ 
sed at length and after discussing the entire issue has been 
settled as follows ; 

That all non-officer supervisory staff as mentioned above 
will be treated a* workmen under the Standing Orders in 
the Guwahati Refinery and with this change, those employees 
will become entitled to over time in lieu of the facilities which 
they were getting as supervisor. There would, however, he 
no change in the nature of their duties, job responsib lilies 
and work requirements as are existing now and these em¬ 
ployees would continue to perform their existing jobs, work 
requirements and hold same responslblities as of now. 
TKe over time Payment will be given as per Cor¬ 
poration’s Rule only for the actual hours of work done 
subject to the maximum of 4 hours in a day. The arrears 
amount of overtime shall be given after adjustment of the 
amount already drawn by the concerned non officer super¬ 
visory employee's for extra duty hours of work done earlier. 

The Case No. 5-C of 1988 pend'ng before the Tribunal 
Is hereby settled. The above payment of over time to the 
non-officer supervisory staff would be effective from 1st 
April, 1988. 

It is therefore prayed that the Reference may be disposed 
of holding that the non-officer supervisory employees 
of the Guwahati Refinery of I.O.C. will be entitled 
to overtime wages as per the Rules of the Corpo¬ 
ration in force and other relief in the Reference 
5-c/88 Referred by Notification No. L-30011 /10/86- 
D.ITI.B-N-B dated 12-10-87 fire hereby given up.

By urder No. L-29012/24/88-D,IlI(B), diqed, the 26th July, 
1988, the Central Government in the Ministry of Labour, 
bus, in exeicise of Ihe powers conferred by clause (d) of 
sub-secuon (1) and sub-section (2A) of Section 10 of the 
industrial Disputes Act, Iy47, referred the following dispute 
foi adjudication to tills Tribunal: 

“Whether the action of the management of M/s. B. 
Kumar and Co. in terminating the services of Shri 
Anant Lai Mahto, Night Guard in their Shovapur 
Mine w.c f, 26-6-79 is justified, If not, to what 
relief the workman 13 entitled ?” 

2. The case of the management of M/s. B. Kumar and 
Co., Shovapur, Rajmahal, as appearing from the written state¬ 
ment received on 9-3-1989 is that Anant Lai Mahto, the 
concerned workman worked as Night Guard of M/s. B. 
Kumar & Co. from 31-8-77 to 9-5-1979. He did not, however, 
report for duly from 10-5-79 and left the service on his 
own accord. He was paid all his dues for the period he 
worked under the company and nothing is left outstanding. 
Hence, the question of terminating his service by the com¬ 
pany docs not arise. 

3. The case of the concerned workman, as appearing in his 
w ritten statement submitted on 30-9-1988, is as follows : 

M/s. U, Kumar & Co,, Shovapur, has many sister concerns, 
such as, M/s. Kaiunpura Mining Corporation, M/s. Jainabad 
Keoline Mine, M/s. Kasba Kaoline Mine and other concerns. 
The concerned workman was appointed as Night Guard in 
M/s. Kasba Kaoline Mine in 1970 and he worked there 
continuously till 24-8-77. He was not given attendance card, 
wage slips, P.E. conti ibution and other facilities. The manage¬ 
ment transferred him from M/s, Kasha Kaoline Mine to 
M/s. B, Kumar & Co., Shovapur Rajmahal on 25-8-77 where 
he worked continuously till 21-2-79. His Form ‘B’ serial 
number is 203 and P.F. Account No. 2179/70. The manage¬ 
ment again transferred him from M/n. B. Kumar and Co. 
lo M/s. Kaiunpura Mining Corporation, Rajmahal on 22-2-79 
and since then he was working there till 21-4-79, But the 
management did not record his atendunce nor did it make 
any payment to hint till 22-6-79. He claimed for his dues 
and the management assured to make payment, but ultimately 
did not. The matter was brought to the notice of Sri D. 
Mishra. General Secretary. Mangalhat Khadan. Mazdoor 
Sangh, Rajmahal. The General Secretary assured him to file

making efforts to get into the employment since he was

question as to whether the employee has abandoned his job 
or not is a question of fact which is to be determined in 
the light of surrounding circumstances. Tt appears from the 
written statement of the concerned workman that he was

AWARD 

The Government of- India. Ministry of Labour, and Reha¬ 
bilitation in exercise of the powers conferred on ihem under 
Section 10,(l)(d) of the I. D. Act, 1947 has referred the 
following dispute to -this Tribunal for adjudication vide 
their Order No. L-20012(379)/gE-D.III (Ah dated, the 
24th March, 1983. 




5232. THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : DECEMBER 1, 1990/ AGRAHA Y ANA 10, 1912 [Part II—Sec. 3(ii)] 


SCHEDULE 

“Whether the action of the management, of Darnoda 
Colliery. Area No, J, Bharat Coking Coal Limited, 
in terminating employment of Stuvashri Gandhi 
Barora and Bhushan Bartia, O.B.K. workers, is 
justified 7 If not, to what relief arc these work¬ 
men entitled 7" 

'l he case of the management is that S/Shri Gandhi Barora 
and Bhushan Baralia were admittedly workng as O.B.R. in 
Uaroru Colliery of JVl/s, B.C.C.L. since betorc nationalisa¬ 
tion. The two concerned workmen who have ra.sed t ie 
present industrial dispute arc not the genuine workmen and 
they have no right to demand employment. The two person* 
posing themselves as Gandhi Barora and Bhushan Bartia 
entered into the service of tho management surreptitiously 
with the connivance of lhe local leaders and the Colliery staff 
and started working in place of and in the name of the 
genuine workmen, S/Shri Gandhi Barora and Bhushan 
Baratia who had left the employment without prior infor¬ 
mation. As soon as the facts of impersonation were detected 
the management issued cfmrgcsheet to the fake workmen 
working in the name ol Gandhi Barora and Bhushan Baralia 
and they submitted their replies denying allegation made 
against them in the charges. Departmental enquiries were" 
held separately into lhe charges against them and they were 
slopped from duties after it was established on domestic 
enquiry that they were impersonators. After the said order 
those workmen left their employment in 1976 and did not 
raise any demand with the management and ultimated their 
case was closed. The case of the management further is 
that some new persons are making attempt to enter into the 
services of the management by impersonating themselves in 
the name of Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia. The dis¬ 
pute has been raised at such a late stage obviously with an 
ulterior motive, Pravakar Patlanaik son of Raghunath Patta- 
naik was impersonting Sltri Gandhi Barora son of Vim 
Barora. Kalia Behera son of Jagunnath Behera was imper¬ 
sonating Bhusan Baratia. The concerned persons in this 
case are not entitled to any relief. 

The case of the workmen is that the two concerned work¬ 
men arft permanent employees of the management of Barora 
Colliery from the period prior to its take over ami subsequent 
nationalisation and they continued in their employment till 
October, 1978. In October, 1978 the two concerned workmen 
were stopped with the management from their duties without 
any notice in any manner whatsoever. The management had 
not clarified their stand as to why they had stopped the two 
concerned workmen from their duties. The concerned work¬ 
men and their union represented the matters several time for 
allowing the concerned workmen to perform their duties or 
to clarify the reason as to why they were not allowed to do 
their duties when the union of the workmen raised industrial 
dispute before ALC(C) Dhanbad, the Agent Damoda Colliery 
in his letter admitted that the concerned workmen were ver¬ 
bally stopped on the alleged ground that they were impersona¬ 
tors. The management had no drawn any proceedings 
against the two concerned workmen in order to establish thaf 
they were impersonators. On the date of lake over of the 
Colliery the names of (he two concerned workmen were in¬ 
cluded in the man power lift which is a sine proof that I hey 
had been working in the Colliery. If by subsequent action of 
the management of the Colliery any change was made in the 
persons concerned there should have been thorough enquiry 
to reveal the state of affairs. The management has no power to 
declare any workman impersonators without taking proper 
aetlbti against them. On the above facts it is prayed by the 
workmen that an Award be passed in favour of the workmen 
and that the two concerned workmen he allowed to join 
their orig'nal jobs with full back wages with conse.qucnlial 
benefits. 

According to the management domestic enqury were laid 
s-^paralely against the two persons who were working in 
the name of Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia and after 
ho'ding domestic enquiry they were found to be impersona¬ 
tors and their work was stopped. Tt will further appear 
from the case of the management that proceedings of the 
domestic enquiry in respect of Gandhi Barora alias Rajan 
Pradhan has been produced. The enquiry proceedings m 
respect of Bints: n Barntia could not be produced oh the 
ground that the proceeding was traceless, ft was- therefore, 


prayed on behalf of the management by suosequent petitions 
that as domestic proceeding against Bhusan Baratia were not 
available and the management my be allowed to address fresh 
evidence before this Tribunal to establish charge against him 
and the said prayer was allowed by the order ot this Tribunal 
dated 6-4-88 and accordingly the management has examined 
M\V-2, So far the case of Gandhi Barora is concerned the 
entire domestic proceeding has been produced by the manage¬ 
ment and it was prayed that it may first be decided as a 
preliminary issue whether the enquiry held against Gandhi 
Barora was fair proper and in accordance wiih the principles 
of natural justice. This prayer of the management was allowed 
by the order No, 27 dated. 29-8-85. The preliminary 
issue was ordered by this 'Jribunal and in order was passed 
on 13-5-88 holding that the enquiry held against Shri Gandui 
Barora Alias Rajan Pradhan was fair proper and in accor¬ 
dance with the principles of natural justice and it was ordered 
that the case of Gandhi Barora will now be heard on the 
materials which arc already in the enquiry proceeding held 
against hint. Thus the hearing of the ca.c ol Gandhi Barora 
on merit on the materials of the enquiry proceeding is to be 
made. The management led evidence in respect of the alle¬ 
gation of impersonation against Bhusan Baratia. 

The points to be decided in the case arc ;■— 

(1) Whether Gandhi Barrora who tas appeared before 
this Tribunal i s the genuine Gandhi Barora. 

(2) Whether the mnogement has been able to establish 
that the charge against Bhusan Baralia of imper¬ 
sonation has been established before this Tribunal 
and 

(3) To what relief, if any are Slv-i Gandhi Barora and 

Bhusan Baratia entitled 7 

The management has examined enquiry officer MW-1 who 
had held domestic enquiry into (he charge against Gandhi 
Barora. The management has further examined MW-2 
Bholanath Paul to establish the allcgation/charge of imper¬ 
sonation against Bhusan Baratia, 

During the evidence in the case the dispute has completely 
melted to the point that the concerned workmen S/JShrl 
Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia who have appeared 
before this Tribunl are the real Gandhi Barora and Bhusan 
Baratia who were working as O.B.R. since befoic natiojialisa- 
tion and that in their absence fake persons in their names 
had entered into the services of the management representing 
themselves as Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia who were 
chargeshectcd and were stopped from their work after holding 
a domestic enquiry into the charge that they were impersonat¬ 
ing the real Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia. 

MW-2 Bholanath Paul hud been examined in the domes’ic 
enquiry in respect of Gandhi Barora. Tt will appears from 
his evidence before the enquiry officer in the enquiry proceed¬ 
ing-Ext. M-3'that Gandhi Barova had made a complaint 
before the Manager, Damoda Colliery on C-7-1975 vide 
Ext. 1 of the enquiryjjrojeeding and thereafter Gandhi Barora 
was ehargesheeted and' a "domestic enquiry was held against 
him, Ext.-l shows that on the basis of lhe comp’aint of 
Gandhi Barora the management orderod to iscuc charge sheet 
for fraud/impersonation against Gandhi Barora alias Rajan 
Pradhan. MW-2 has stated before this Tribunal in his cross- 
examination that he had deposed in the departmental enquiry 
against Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia in which the 
management had raised the question of their impersonation. 
He has slated that the two persons of the name of Gondhi 
Barora apd Bhusan "Baratia were working in the Colliery who 
were detected as impersonators. He has stated that he identi¬ 
fied the real Gandhi Barova and Bhusan Baratia as 
O.B.R. of Damoda Colliery. The real Gandhi Barora and 
Bhusan Baratia were present in the Court room and this 
witness MW-2 identified them before this Tribunal that they 
are the cenuine Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia. Ho 
also identified their photo' on the affidavits attested by the 
Notary Public, Dhanbad Which has been marked Ext. W-l 
and W-2 in this case. On referring to the evidence of 
Bholftnafh Paul before the enquiry officer which is "recorded 
at page 17 of the enquiry ' proceeding Ext, M-3 it will 
appear that Bholanath Paul' was working as the Attendance 
Clerk In the Colliery in which Gandhi Barora anfi Bhusan- 
Baratia were working. Tn the enquiry proceeding relating to

Gandhi Barora alias Rajan Pradhan this witness identified 
the complainant, namely, Gandhi Barora (who had filed the 
complaint dated 6-7-75 Ext. 1) as the real Gandhi Baroda. 
Me had slated that the chargesbeeted workman was Rajan 
Pradhan. Besides Bholanath Paul the management had also 
examined one Bansha Pradhan before the enquiry officer who 
also identified the complainant as real Gandhi Barora and 
the chargeshected workman was identified as Rajan Pradhan, 
The complainunt Gandhi Barora was also oxamined by the 
management in the cliquey who has stated that since one yea r 
prior to take over he was working in Ramgarh Jhura Colliery 
and that in January or Februafy he went to his village as 
Colliery had been locked out and subsequently when he came 
and Wanted to joifi his duty he has not allowed to join and 
thereafter he filed a complaint. In view of the fact that the 
real Gandhi Barora had been identified by the management's 
own witness there is no reason to disbelieve that Gaiidhi 
Barora whose photo was attached on the affidavit Ext, M-l 
is the real Gandhi Barora who had been working in the Mines 
and that during his absence fake person Had surreptitiously 
entered into the service in the name of Gandhi Barora and 
as subsequently removed from service after holding a domestic 
enquiry. Ext. Wl-3 is the notice issued by the ALC(C), 
Dhanbad to the G.M, Barora Area No. I and the Secretary, 
RCMS, Dhanbad dated 13/16-8-82. It will show that the 
ALC(C) had taken up the matter in conciliation on the 
petition of the workmen dated 7-8-82, This industrial dispute 
related to the alleged stoppage of work of Gandhi Barora 
and Bhusan Baratia, O.B.R. with effect from October, 1976. 
Para 7 and 8 of the W.S. of the management will show 
that the persons against whom domestic enquiry was field 
left their employment and did not raise any demand with the 
management. It is further stated that this dispute was raised 
at late stage by some new persons who are making attempt 
to enter into the service by impersonating themselves. It 
is obvious therefore that the present dispute was not raised 
on. behalf of the fake Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia 
against whom domestic enquiry was held and were removed 
from service and that the present dispute was raised by 
other persons in the name of Gandhi Barora and Bhusan 
Baratia, I have already discussed above that it has been 
amply established now that Gandhi Barora in respect of 
whom this dispute has been raised was the real Gandhi Barora 
and that no proceeding against him was gone into by the 
management. Tt is stated by the management in their re¬ 
joinder to the W.S. in para 8 that the workmen whose names 
were in ihe man power list were not stopped from their duties 
and they had voluntarily abandoned their service. It is clear 
therefore that the real Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia 
bad not actlllly been stopped from their duties and that no 
action had been taken against them by the management. It 
will further appear from the enquiry proceeding that it was 
on the complaint of Gandhi Barora that the enquiry proceed¬ 
ing was started and the management had taken advaotgpe of 
his evidence in identifying the real persons and got rid of 
fake Gandhi Barora but did not actually give employment 
to real Gandhi Barora who had appeared before the manage¬ 
ment in July, 1975 during the domestic enquiry. 

In view of the discussions made ahove I hold that Gandhi 
Barora who has appeared before this Tribunal and whose 
photo is attached with affidavit Ext. W-1 is the real Gandhi 
Barora who was the workmen of the management. 

Point No.. 2 

WW-2 who was (he Attendance Clerk Bholanath Paul 
in Ramgarh Jharia Colliery knew Bhusan Baratia who was 
residing in the same Colony in which he was residing. He 
has stated Titat both Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia were 
working as O.B.R. and they used to come to his residence. 
He has stated that when Shri R. K. Khandai, Manager mode 
enquiry it was learnt that sonic other two persons were" 
working : n their place and that MW-2 wRh called bv the 
Manager to identify the real persons. In cross-examination 
he h:‘ S identified the persons present in the Court and whose 
photo is contained on the affidavit Ext. W-2 as Bhusan 
Baratia. This fact of identification is not now being challen¬ 
ged by the management, There is nothing on the record to 
falsify MWi 7. Accordingly T hold that Bhusan Baratia whose 
photo Is pasted on Ext. W-2 and is attested by the Notary 
Public Dhanbad is the real Bhusan Baratia who was y orking 
ns OB.R. Since before nationalisation. Tt has clearly been 
established that the said Bhusan Baratia who was present 


In the Court is the real Bhusan Baratia find" as the manage¬ 
ment has not taken any action against him and has not 
removed him from service is entitled to join his duly. 

Point No. 3- 

Shri S. Bose appearing on behalf of the workmen has 
very clearly conceded that he would not pray for back 
wages in respect of the concerned wotkman and that he would 
be satisfied if some lump sum amount is allowed to them for 
the period of their idleness since they had appeared beforo 
the management to join - their duties in 1975 or 1976 
ftlong with the order directing the management to allow 
them to join their duties. It appeals, no doubt that the 
two workmen, namely, Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia 
had absented and had reported only in the year 1975 or 
1976 and had not worked for management since them. It 
was only because of their long absence that some fake perseons 
had entered into the employment in their name and the 
management had also suffered on that account. As such tho 
ends of justice will be met if lump sum amount of Rs. 5000 
(Rupees five thousand) only is paid to each of the con¬ 
cerned workman. 

In the result T hold that flic management had not termi¬ 
nated the services of the real Gandhi Barora and Bhusan 
Baratia and that the management was justified in terminating 
the employment of the fake Gandhi Barora and Bhusan 
Baratia who Were Working in place of real Gandhi 
Barora and Bhusan Baratia. I further hold that the manage¬ 
ment was not justified in not allowing tho real Gandhi Barora 
and Bhusan Baratia tojoin their duties when they had repor¬ 
ted in the Colliery. The management is, therefore, directed 
to allow Gandhi Barora and Bhusan Baratia to join their 
duties after verifying them from photo on Ext. W-1 and Ext. 
W-2 within one month from the date of publication of the 
Award. The managemfent should also pay each of them a 
lump sum amount of Rs, 5000 soon after their joining. 

This is my Award. 

I. N. SINHA, Presiding Officer

By Order No. L-200I2(154)|84-I.R. (Coal-1), dated, the

of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947, referred the following 
dispute for adjudication to this Tribunal : 

“Whether the action of the management of Simlabahal 
Colliery Bhalgora Area of M|g, Bharat Coking Coal 
Ltd., in terminating Shri Ram Prasad Barhi, Night 
Guard, from service w.e.f, 12-11-1983 vide its

the workman entitled 7”, 

2.. The case of the management of Simlabahal Colliery 
of Bhalgora Area of M|s. B.C.C. Ltd,, as appearing from 
the written statement submitted, details apart,' is as follows: 

The present reference is not legally maintainable. The 
concerned workman was the Night Guard on duty in the 
night shift of 12-12-82 commencing at midnight of 12-12-82 
and endihg at 8.00 a.m, of 13-12-82, to guard the cash room 
of Bhalgora office. During his duty hours, th outside door 
of the cash room was opened after breaking the bolt with 
a hammer by thieves. Thereafter the thieves entered into 
cash room opened the iron safe by removing the ring. 
Ultimately the thieves decamped with the money amounting 
to- Rs.. 2,03,337.10- (Rupees Two lakhs three thousand five 
hundred thirty seven and paise ten only) from the iron safe 
of the cash loom. There was eVery reason to suspect that 
theft was committed with the active participation or con¬ 
nivance of the concerned Workman. Ah F.I.R. Was lodged 
to . the police and in the course of search and seizure huge 
amount of stolen money was recovered from some work¬ 
men and involvemeut of the concerned workman could not 
be ruled out. Accordingly to the police initiated criminal 
proceedings against the concerned workmen aloogwith others. 
The management held preliminary enquiry and recorded 
the statement of the concerned worknjgn. From his own 
statement it was revealed that he committed serious mis¬ 
conduct of negligence of duty resulting in huge loss of lakhs 
of rupees and that he dishonestly cause huge loss to the 
management. The job of Night Guard of the cash room 
is of great importance and sleeping on duty on leaving the 
place of duty and going out may result in theft of huge 
amount. Therefore the Night Guard of cash room is ex¬ 
pected to know the consequence of the act of commission 
ard omission and in case of causing loss to property of 
th > cash room shall be deemed to cause loss knowingly. 
Aiyway, he was issued with a chargesheet dated 13-12-82 
alleging misconduct of dishonesty and negligence of duty. 
He submitted his reply dated 15-12-82 denying allegations 
levelled against him. In reply to the chargesheet be took 
tfie defence that be was deputed to guard the Manager's 
office and not the cash room of Bhalgora office. Shri B. D. 
Singh, the then Dv, Personnel Manager of Kustore Area 
was appointed as Enquiry Officer to conduct the depart¬ 
mental enquiry into the chargesheet Issued to the concerned 
workman. The enquiry was conducted In presence of the 
concerned workman. Management’s representative produced 
documents, namely, the chargesheet, the F.I.R., earlier 
statement of the chargeshccted workman and his reply to 
’he chargesheet. The witnesses for the management were 
examined In nrcsence of the concerned workman and he 
was given full opportunity to cross-examine the witnesses. 
He was also given fall opportunity to give his own state¬ 
ment and to produce his defence witness. The enquiry was 
hold m accordance with the- prlnoiles of- natural justice. 
The Enquiry Officer submitted his. report holding the con¬ 
cerned workman guilty of the. misconduct charged against 


hint. The enquiry report, proceeding and all connected 
papers were exahmied at various levels and' approval of his 
dismissal fiom service was ohtained from the Goneral 
Managef||Chicf Mining Engineer. Thereafter the concerned 
Workman was dismissed trom service by letter dated 
12-11-83 with immediate effect. 

3. The case - oT the sponsoring union, as appearing from 
the written statement submitted on behalf of the concerned 
workman, briefly stated, is as follows : 

'Ihe concerned workman. Ram Prasad Barhi, was work¬ 
ing in permanent |>ost as Night Guard in Bhalgora Colliery. 
He joined his service in that capacity and was dismissed from 
service With effect fiom 12-11-83. He was served with a 
chargesheet dated 13-12-82 alleging misconduct of disonesty 
in connection with employer’s business, and neglect of 
work. He submitted his explanation on 14-4-83 strqngly 
refuting and denying the charges. But the management, 
without holding any proper enquiry in accordance with the 
principles of natural justice and without any legal evidence 
dismissed him frCm service illegally, wrongly and lu a 
arbitrary manner. Fof the same cause of action a criminal 
case was instituted against him in Dhanbad Court. The 
Trying Magistrate acquitted him of the charge and held 
that he was an innocent workman and no offence was com¬ 
mitted by him. Thereafter the matter in dispute was raised 
before the Asstt. Labour Commissioner (Q, Dbanbad-lV, 
and during conciliation proceeding the Asstt. Labour Com¬

to reinstate him in service with back wages, but the manage¬

sulted in the present reference for. adjudication by the ap¬

or dismissal be act aside and the concrened .workman be 
rc-lnstated in service with full back wages.

9 . l n ihe present case a preliminary enquiry was made 
by the management before issuing chargesheet against the

approval for dismissal of the concerned workman 
anti mefealter the General Manager had given his 
approval tor dismissal of the concerned workman 
v.uc rcxt, ivi-/. the Suo-Area Manager only carried

tne General Manager and as such it cannot be sain

The workmen have tiled certified copy of judge¬ 
ment of Criminal Appeal No. 360J 79 dated 2-8-85 
passed by the 4th Addl. Session-, Judge, Dhanbnd. 
The judgement will show r that he was originally con¬ 
victed for the offence under Section 304 I.P.C. and 
was sentenced to undergo R.l. for 5 years by the 
Asstt. Sessions Judge, Dhanbad. On arpeal the con¬ 
cerned workman was actuated and his appeal was 
allowed. A judgement of Criminal case shows 2 
things namely that th:re was prosecution against the 
concerned workman on certain charges and the pro¬ 
secution ended in a certain way. The reason and] 
or other finding of fact may not he admissible. It 
cannot be said that principles of natural justice re¬ 
quired that the management must wait for fh : 
decision of the criminal case befoie taking action 
against an employee. The matter for consideration 
in a criminal case are different from the matters 
which have to be considered in a domestic enquiry 
on the evidence led befor; the enquiry officer. The 
enquiry officer has to decide the case on the evi¬ 
dence before him and so far this case is concerned 
there was no existence o : the result of the criminal 
case before the enquiry o ll; r when he had held 
the domestic enquiry'. Even this Tribunal cannot 
consider the result of the criminal case to arrive at 
the finding' in a domestic enquiry was decided oil 
the evidence which was adduced before the enquiry 
officer. I hold, therefore, that the result of the 
criminal trial [criminal appeal cannot be used to 
show that the charges against the concerned work¬ 
man in the domestic enquiry has not been esta¬ 
blished. 

Ill the result 1 hold that the demand ot the Coal 
Mines Engineering workers association for reinstate¬ 
ment of the concerned workman Shri Anandi 
Prasad, Ex-Fitter Helper of Angarpathra colliery of 
BCCL is not justified and consequently the con¬ 
cerned workman is entitled to no relief. 

This is my Award.

AWARD 

IJTie industrial dispute between the above parties 
was referred to this. Court for adjudication by the 
Government of India, Ministry of Labour, New 
Delhi , as per Order No. L-170l2[82-D. 1V(A) 
dated 9th January, 1987. the issue covered by the 
reference order is the following :— 

“Whether the action of the management of 
the United India Eire & General Insur¬ 
ance Company Limited (now known as 
United India Insurance Company) Eran- 
kulam in terminating the services of 
Kumari K. Meera Bai with effect from 
1-11-1974 is: justified? If not, to what 
relief is the workman concerned entitled”? 

2, The Union has filed a claim statement stating- 
as follows :— 

Kumari Meera Bai applied for the post of 
typist in the United India Fire & Genera)! 
Insurance Company Limited and she was. 
appointed by the Company in their various, 
units temporarily as (Typist between the 
following periods :— 

From 14-6-1973 for 8 wedts. 

From 12-11-1973 for 4 vefiks. 

From 9-1-1974 for 8 weeks.

j4?Aill u—3.SC. Uyil/J. 


From 22-4-1974 for S weeks. 

From 2-8-1974 ter. 14-9-1974. 

From 3-10-19 74 to 15-10-1974. 

From 26-10-1974 to 31-10-1974. 

7 1 he period so calculated together works out to 
more than 240 days, of service within a period of 
i2 month in the Company, The last date of her 
employment came to an end on 31-10-1974. Inspire 
of the mandatory provisions of dec. 2oF of the 
{. D. Act the Compafiy did not pay any compen- 
sat.on to her or was the required notice given to 
her. The Union took up the matter with man¬ 
agement and ultimately in the Southern Region 
nearly 30 employees who were similarly situated 
were absorbed m.-e permanent service, z> m.. xiumaii 
Meera Bai was left out. She requested the Chair- 
num-cum-Managing Director for reinstatement in 
service. Inspite of series of correspondence and 
repnescntaCipns there was no response from the 
Company, the break in the service of the workmen 
was not occasioned due to any fault of her but due 
to the actions of the Company in creating an arti¬ 
ficial break Meera Bai had continuous service 
coming within, the definition of the term in Sec¬ 
tion 25-B of the l.D. Act and thereby enti.led to 
ail privileges under Chapter VA of the l.D. Act. 

I The termination, of services of the concerned work¬ 
man and the denial of employment, to her being in. 
violation of the l.D. Act, are void and liable to 
he declared so. 

3. The Management has filed objection contend¬ 
ing as follows. :— 

The workman Kumars K. Meera Bai has not 
been continously in employment for 240 
days or more at any time within in period 
of 12 months. Hence she is not emilled 
to any of the reliefs claimed in the claim 
statement. Termination of service of 
workman who T casual wo i km an 
will not constitute retrenchment. 

. There was no artificial break in service 
as alleged. On the other hand, she was 
only a casual worker. In the United India 
Fire and General Insurance Company 
Ltd., 26 existing Insurance Companies 
were merged. Till the merger of the 
Companies, there were multiplicity of 
branches of the erstwhile Companies' 
Units and to clear off the work load 
employees were appointed on casual 
basis. Kumari Meera Bai is one of such 
employees. She has r.ot worked for 240 
days, for a period of 12 calender months 
proceeding 31-10-74. So Sec. 25F of the 
l.D. Act is. not attracted. Casual emp- 
joyees arc not temporary employees arc 
there was no particular period of employ’ 
merit. They were not given any appoint¬ 
ment order. The Nationalisation Act em¬ 
powered ‘ the Central Government to 
frame scheme for nationalisations and 
revisions, of pay scales and other condi¬ 
tions of service- of supervisory .clerical ami 
subordinate staff scheme _ 1974. It regu¬ 
lates tile' whole service condition 'of cleri¬ 
cal, and' iitpeivisbry 'statt right from 
categorisation-till supertmtRiatiofl. It does 


no apply, to casual employees or - part-kme

S.O, 3262.—In pursuance of .Section 17 of the 
Industrial Dispute Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the 
Central Government hereby publishes the award of 
the Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Bombay 
as shown in the ahnexure in the Industrial di pute 
between the employers in relation to the Delia Bank 
and their workmen, which was received by the 
Central Government on 6-11-90.,

The Central Government by their order No. L- 
12012|48|89-D-2(A) dated 18-8-1989 have referred 
the following industrial dispute to this Tribunal for 
adjudication under Section 10(1) (d) of the Industrial 
Disputes Act :— 

“Whether the action of the management of Dena 
Bank in terminating the services of Shri 
Satyawan B. Naik and not con idering him 
for further employment while recruiting 
fresh hands under Section 25H of the In¬ 
dustrial Disputes Act is justified 7 If not, 
to what relief is the workman entitled ?” 

2. The General Secretary of All India Dena Bank 
Staff Federation filed the statement of claim (Ex. 2) 
challenging the said action of the Bank Management. 
The Regional Manager of the Bank filed their written 
statement (Ex. 3) in support of the action of the 
Bank Management. The necessary issues have been 
framed .at Ex. 4. 


I’fwrmr-anoia (23)‘» ( »-rot. grr.-IJ 


New Delhi, the 8th November, 1990 

S.O. 3263.—In exercise of the powers conferred 
by sub-section (3) of section 1 of the Employees 
State In urance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948), the Central 
Government hereby appoints the 16th November, 
1990 as the date on which the provisions of Chapter 
IV (except sections 44 and 45 which have already 
been brought into force) and Chapters V and VI 
(except ub-section (1) of section 76 and sections 77, 
78, 79 and 81 which have already been brought into 
force) of the said Act shall come into force in the fol¬ 
lowing areas in the State of Punjab namely ■— 


S. No. Revenue Vi 11''go IDd B"st Tchsil Dist ict 

NO. 


3, Thereafter while the reference. was at the stage 
of evidence, both the parties arrived at a settlement 
and filed a joint application containing the terms of 
settlement (Ex. 5) which run thus :— 

‘T. That in the meanwhile a full time vacancy 
has arisen in the Vassco-da-Gama branch 
of the Bank in the Subordinate cadre and 
as submitted in the Bank’s written state¬ 
ment the workman Shri S. B. Naik can 
therefore be taken into the Bank v regular 
employment in the subordinate cadre, 

2. That the appointment of the said workman 
would be from a prospective date without 
backwages. The Bank has already initiated 
the process for the appointment of the 
workman.” 

3. I find that this settlement is quite in the 
interests of both the parties. Therefore, the Award 
mu' t be, and is drawn in terms of the said settlement. 
Dt. 20-10-90.

2. The case as made out in the written statement 
by the union sponsoring the cause of the Concerned 
workmen is briefly as follows ; The Food Corpora¬ 
tion of India (Corporation in brief) has its Head 
Office at New Delhi and four Zonal Offices at Calcutta, 
Bombay, New Delhi and Madras. There are several 
Regional Offices under the Zonal Manager and 
there are District Offices in each district and several 
depots where foc^dgrains are stored. Several Handl¬ 
in'? Mazdoors are employed by the Corporation in 
each depot and thev generally work in a gang of 
14 composed of 1 Sardar, 1 Mondal and 12 Maz¬ 
doors. There are other worker- known as Ancillary 
workers who generally perform the work of sweeping, 
‘fitch my. Collection of loo c e food grains and other 
techn'cal operation and also nerform the work of 
nlacement of wooden cratesldarma matfinn at the 
tunc of loading and unloading the food grams hags. 
The H-indh'nc A'azdonrc ?»nr) flip Ancillary workers 
o°rtiVu1arlv m Assam Region, having the denot n f 
the Coroorafion at Gnwahati. Ohinamara. and Haim’, 
have Been nrp*f'rirr the waupo. incentive waves, over¬ 
time CPF. homw ind vratmtv and other fringe bene¬ 
fits nc ner the Award dated 10-10-1974 of Mr. K. 
K. Mitra. 


3. The Comorat’on atmointed the ronce r ned work¬ 
men numhpr<ncr 76 nc mentioned in thp cdiedide to 
the rpfprence s AneWurv worker- at Ouwatigtt Flenof

35 workman have heen nerformin? the same and

similar nature of work which are being performed 
by the Ancillary workmen appointed in the same 
depot in the year 1973 but the concerned 35 Ancillary 
workmen are not being given the wages and other 
benefits as per the Award of Mr. K. K. Mitra. The 
Corporation has violated the principle of equal 
remuneration for equal work by not giving the wages 
and other fringe benefits to the 35 concerned work¬ 
men as are being given to other Ancillary workmen 
1973. So the concerned w'orkmen through their 
union demanded the similar wages and other fringe 
benefits as are being paid to other Ancillary workmen 
who arc doing the similar nature of work but to no 
effect, Accordingly the dispute arose between the 
Corporation and the union sponsoring the cause of 
the concerned workmen, resulting in the present 
referende. 


4. The case as made out by the Corporation in 
their written statement and rejoinder is as follows. 
The present reference is not maintainable and that 
the union which has sponsored the cause of the con¬ 
cerned workmen has got no locus standi to raise the 
dispute and that accordingly the Tribunal has got no 
jurisdiction to adjudicate the dispute involved in 
the present referene. It has been contended by the 
Corporation that 35 concerned workmen as named 
in the schedule to the reference are the casual workers 
and not the Ancillary workmen as claimed by the 
Union. The Ancillary workmen have their status- 
different from that of the casual workers and their 
nature of work is also different. The Ancillary 
workmen are the departmental regular workmen but 
the casual workers are not to, The casual workers 
work on daily basis as and when required by the 
Corporation. Such being the position, the concerned 
workmen being the casual workers are not entitled 
to the similar wages and fringe benefit- as arc paid 
to the Ancillary workmen. The Corporation has 
denied that the concerned workmen numbering 35 
as mentioned in the schedule to the reference have 
been working since 1976. Tt has been further con¬ 
tended that the Corporation has not violat' d any 
principle of equal pay for eoual work hv not owin ' 7 
the similar wage- and fringe benefits to the concerned 
workmen as are beintr paid to the Ancillary workmen 
as their nature of work is different. Tt lias been 
further contended that the concerned workmen bein ' 7 
casual workers do not come under the purview of 
the Award of Mr. K. K. Mitra. Tire Corporation 
accordingly has not been unimtified in derivin ' 1 
similar wages and fringe benefits to the concerned 
workmen as are being paid to the Ancillary workmen. 

5. Both sides have given evidence and produced 
some documents in support of their respective state¬ 
ments. 

6. As regards the Corporation’s contention that 
the reference is not maintainable and that the union 
has got no locus standi, the Corporation has not 
pressed such contention at the time of henriro and 
ha - not produced any evidence in surwni-t of such 
contention. Such being the position. Corporation’s 
such entention bnffi with 'regard to the main¬ 
tainability of the reference and locus/ standi of the 
union stands rejected.

it. Although in view of the submission of Mr.

no more required to adjudicate whether tne con¬ 
cerned 2U workmen were Ancillary workmen or 
ca uai workers till belore 1-1-1909 out 1 mid nom 
tne evidence as given m the instant ease mat ail 
cue concerned 2u workmen since tneir appointment 
were doing tneir work lor more tnan Z4<J days in me 
calendar year and mat mey were cuing me same 
nature of work as is being done by tne Ancillary 
workmen. MW-2 Mr. H. S. Banga wno is the Deputy 
Manager, Administration and l. R. in the Regional 
Uincc ol Uuwahati has stated in his evidence that 
Ancidary workers are the departmental regular wor¬ 
kers. Mvv-2 JVlr. Banga has admitted m his deposition 
with reierence to the Bonus Register Ext. M-2|l that 
concerned workman Motilal Kewat worked tor 35b 
days in the year 1978-1979 and that Ramesh 
Ciymdra Kalita worked for 340 days in that year. 
The other Register* have not been produced. None ot 
the two witnesses who have been examined on the 
side of the Corporation has stated that the concerned 
workmen did not work continuously and did not work 
240 days in any calendar year. On the other hand 
WW-1 P. R. Lias has stated in his evidence that the 
concerned 20 workmen have been working continu¬ 
ously since their appointment. It appears that this 
witness WW-1 P. R. Das out of his exuberation lias 
stated that ail the concerned 20 workmen including 
himself have been working since 1976, although the 
statement showing the engagment of the casual wor¬ 
kers Ext. W-12 which has been admitted and produ¬ 
ced by the union shows that many of the concerned 
20 workmen including WW-1 him self were not ap¬ 
pointed in 1976, The said list shows that only 5 of 
the said 20 workmen were appointed in 1976 and 
that WW-1 P. R. Das was appointed in 1978. Be 
that as it may, the evidence of WW-1 and the evidence 
of MW-1 show that the concerned 20 workmen used 
to do almost the same nature of work as was being 
done by the Ancillary workmen, although the con¬ 
cerned workmen were appointed first as casual wor¬ 
kers. It has already been shown that the aforesaid 20 
workmen whose claim is being considered by this Tri¬ 
bunal were working continuously and were working 
for more than 240 days in every calendar year since 
their appointment. The Corporation’s letter dated 
29-10-81 Ext. W-2 to the District Manager of FCI 
Jorhat regarding the engagement of casual labour spells 
out if a casual labour works for 90 days in a year he 
is deemed to be eligible for rcgularisntion of his emp¬ 
loyment as per FCI Rules. On due consideration ol 
the nature of work and the period of work done by 
the concerned workmen I find that they are eligible 


to gci tne similar wages and Kings benefits as arc 
being paid u> Uie Ancuiary worKmen who ate none 
else man tne legular workmen ot tne corporation. 

12. mere is no dispute to tbe tact and it appears 
,ioiu tne corpoiaUun s teller dated s-o-iimfi to tne 
union rod, M-l mat me to casual amours including 
tne concerned 20 workmen were agreed to be maue 
regular and it appears from the evidence as also from 
me submission nom nom sides that tne concerned 2U 1 
workmen were made regular from 1-1-1959 on the 
basis ol the said letter. VvW-1 P- K. Idas lias stated in 
ins evidence that tne concerned workmen accepted the 
rebel irom l-i-i9o9 under protest us ifietr claim for 
relief trom the date of then appointment. 

13. in view of what has been discussed above, 1 
find tnat the concerned workmen are entitled to get 
tne similar wages and fringe benents as are applicable 
to the regular Ancillary workmen, irom before 
1-1-1989. Now die question is from which dute they 
are entitled to such relief. According to Mr. Mitra, 
the learned Counsel for the union such relief should 
be gives to the concerned workmen Irom tne date ol 
their appointment and according to Mr. Roy, die 
learned Advocate for the Corporation, such relief may 
be given to the concerned 2t) workmen either from 
the date of the reference or from the date of their 
demand, if any. Placing relience on die decision in 
the case of Hydro (Engineering) Pvt. Ltd. v. work¬ 
men, reported in AIR 1969 SC 182 and in the case 
ol Bharat Cotton Growers’ Co-operadve Spinning 
Mills Ltd. v. Miraj Taluka Girni Kamgar Sangh, re¬ 
ported in 1979 (1) LLJ 487, I find no least hesitation 
to hold that the concerned 20 workmen should get the 
relief from the date of their demand, if there be any 
before the date of the reference. It is an undisputed 
fact and the union’s letter d a t £ d 7lh September, 1981 
to the Corporation Ext. W-7 shows that the union 

sponsoring the cause of the concerned workmen made 
their demand for die same wages and fringe benefits 
as arc paid to the Ancillary workers for the concerned 
workmen first on 7tl. September, I98i. It could not 
be shown before the Tribunal that prior to the afore¬ 
said date the union made any demand for such relief 
to the Corporation, I therefore find that the concer¬ 
ned 20 workmen as named below are entitled to the 
similar wages and fringe benefits as are applicable 
to the regular Ancillary workmen from 7th Septem¬ 
ber, 1981 and make the Award to that effect.

3. Ihe case of the workmen in brief is that 
ihey were workmen employed in Suraxaciihar Coi-

4. According to the management, the reference

publicity regarding recruitment and called upon ail 
ex-employees if any available to apply for the same. 

5. A Committee was constituted to scrutinise 
the names. Several employees applied for the post 
and after secruliny those who were found fit were 

6. Shri Sukumar and Shri Ralnlal, the workmen 
concerned, did not apply for the post nor did they 
appear for interview. Had they applied and appea¬ 
red before the Committee, their case would also 
have been considered. Usual practice in such cases 
was to put the notice in the Notice Board for get¬ 
ting' publicity. Since thereafter there is no vacancy. 
The Colliery has surplus manpower hence they 
cannot be given employment. In any case, they 
have no right to employment because they had 
worked for a few days. 

7. In his rejoinder the management repeated the 

same facts further stating that it is not within 
their knowledge that these workmen were workmen 
of the management or they had employed them. 
Management had received 144 applications, out of 
them 51 candidates appeared in the interview and 
31 were selected. Only those candidates who ap¬ 
peared and were selected in interview were ap¬ 
pointed. 1 





526S THE GAZETTE OFINDJA : DECEMBER ], 1990/AGRAIIAYAN 10, 19)2 [Part U— Sec. 3(ii)] 


ij. Management has no record of the fact that 
these workmen were the workmen of the manage¬ 
ment or they ever worked with the management, 
workmen have not produced any document to 
show that they were workmen of the management, 
fc'heir case is, therefore, liable to be rejected. 

9. Reference was the issue in this case. 

10. No evidence was led by either party. Two 
documents viz. Notice Ex. M|i and Ex. M] 2 were 
not in dispute between the parties, according to 
wmcli the old casual workers who were retrenched 
were called for interview, jlnese documents have 
been displayed on the notice board at various 
places as given therein. 

i 1. There being no specific denial of the fact 
that the workmen concerned were ex-casuai wor¬ 
kers of the management and that they had worked 
tor 50 to 100 days uo proof is necessary in this 
regard. That apart, the matter was considered 
berore the reference was made and these workmen 
were treated as ex-casual workers of the manage¬ 
ment and therefore in view of the judgement dated 
2-8-1989 of the Madhya Pradesh Eligh Court at 
Jabalpur in M.P. No. 2958188 Azad Koyla Shra- 
mik Sablta Vs. Western Coalfields Etd. this Tribu¬ 
nal shall not go beyond the reference in this cir¬ 
cumstances ot the case to find out wliether 
these workers were ex-casual workers of the man¬ 
agement. 

12. (The only question that remains to be ans¬ 
wered is whether the workmen concerned had pro¬ 
per notice of the vacancies to be employed as per 
Sec. 25-H of the l.D. Act. 

13. It is true that the definition of the workmen 
under Sec. 2(s) of the l.D. Act covers the work¬ 
men concerned because it docs not discriminate 
the workmen whether temporary, casual or who had 
not completed one year's continuous service hence 
they would be deemed to be workmen under the 
l.D. Act. 

14. Having persued the Industrial Disputes (Cent¬ 
ral) Rules 1957, Rule 77 and 78 prescribe for 
maintenance of seniority list of workmen and for 
re-employment of retrenched workmen and mode 
of service to retrenched workmen respectively, but 
these Rules are governed by Rule 76 according to 
which these rules would apply to those retrenched 
workmen only who have been in continuous service 
of not less than one year under him and this defini¬ 
tion of ‘workman' has been extended to workmen 
referred to in Rules 77 & 78 of the Industrial Dis¬ 
putes (Central) Rules 1957. 

15. By implication it follows that the provisions 
of Rule 76 to 78 of the l.D. (Central) Rules 
would apply to the workmen who have completed 
one year's continuous service as laid down in Sec. 
25B of the l.D. Act. It follows that these provi¬ 
sions have been specificially left out to be applied 
to the workman who has not completed one year's 
continuous service. 

16. Learned Counsel for the workmen lias 
failed to specifically point out as to what other 


mode would have been applied for intimating the 
workmen concerned because according to the man¬ 
agement there is no record of these casual workers 
who had worked for a very short period. The man¬ 
agement as per Ex. M|l and Ex.Mj2had displayed 
tlte notice at various places as is evident from 
these documents and it would be deemed to be a 
sufficient notice to the workmen concerned. That 
being the case, the workmen having failed to ap¬ 
pear before the management and join, they are 
not entitled to relief as claimed. Thus it cannot be 
said that the demand of the Union to re-employ 
Shri Sukumar and Shri Ratnlal, ex-casual workers, 
is justified. They are not entitled to any relief. 

17. Reference is answered accordingly. No order 
as to costs. 

V. N, SHURLA, Presiding Officer
